| `FC09-C07-U058` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §5, source L19394 | Swapping summation and integration is legal, for the same reason as in the case of the Riemann zeta function (see p. 422). We view the function $$ Z _ {\infty} (s) = \| d _ {K} \| ^ {s / 2} \pi^ {- n s / 2} \Gamma_ {K} (s / 2) = \| d _ {K} \| ^ {s / 2} L _ {X} (s) $$ as the “Euler factor at infinity” of the zeta function $\zeta(\mathcal{K}, s)$ (see §4, p. 455) and define $$ Z (\mathfrak {K}, s) = Z _ {\infty} (s) \zeta (\mathfrak {K}, s). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U059` | Proposition (5.5) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19442 | (5.5) Proposition. The function $Z(\mathfrak{K}, 2s)$ is the Mellin transform $$ Z (\mathfrak {K}, 2 s) = L (f, s) $$ of the function $$ f (t) = f _ {F} (\mathfrak {a}, t) = \frac {1}{w} \int_ {F} \theta (\mathfrak {a}, i x t ^ {1 / n}) d ^ {*} x, $$ where $w = \# \mu(K)$ denotes the number of roots of unity in $K$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U060` | Lemma (5.6) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19482 | (5.6) Lemma. The fundamental domain $F$ of $S$ has the following volume with respect to $d^{*}x$ : $$ \operatorname{vol} (F) = 2 ^ {r - 1} R, $$ where r is the number of infinite places and R is the regulator of K (see chap. I, (7.5)). | `FC09-C01-U060` |
| `FC09-C07-U061` | Lemma (5.7) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19538 | (5.7) Lemma. The lattice $\Gamma'$ in $\mathbf{R}$ which is dual to the lattice $\Gamma = \mathfrak{a}$ is given by $$ { } ^ { * } \Gamma ^ { \prime } = ( \mathfrak { a d } ) ^ { - 1 } , $$ where the asterisk denotes the involution $(x_{\tau}) \mapsto (\overline{x}_{\tau})$ on $K_{R}$ and d the different of $K\|Q$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U062` | Proposition (5.8) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19560 | (5.8) Proposition. The functions $f_{F}(\mathfrak{a}, t)$ satisfy the transformation formula $$ f _ {F} \left(\mathfrak {a}, \frac {1}{t}\right) = t ^ {1 / 2} f _ {F ^ {- 1}} \left((\mathfrak {a d}) ^ {- 1}, t\right), $$ and one has $$ f _ {F} (\mathfrak {a}, t) = \frac {2 ^ {r - 1}}{w} R + O (e ^ {- c t ^ {1 / n}}) \quad \text { for } t \to \infty , c > 0. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U063` | Theorem (5.9) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19636 | (5.9) Theorem. The function $$ Z (\mathfrak {K}, s) = Z _ {\infty} (s) \zeta (\mathfrak {K}, s), \quad \operatorname{Re} (s) > 1, $$ $Z_{\infty}(s) = \|d_K\|^{s / 2}\pi^{-ns / 2}\Gamma_K(s / 2)$ , admits an analytic continuation to $\mathbb{C} \setminus \{0,1\}$ and satisfies the functional equation $$ Z (\mathfrak {K}, s) = Z \left(\mathfrak {K} ^ {\prime}, 1 - s\right), $$ where the ideal classes $\mathfrak{K}$ and $\mathfrak{K}'$ correspond to each other via $\mathfrak{K}\mathfrak{K}' = [\mathfrak{d}]$ . It has simple poles at $s = 0$ and $s = 1$ with residues $$ - \frac {2 ^ {r}}{w} R, \quad r e s p. \frac {2 ^ {r}}{w} R. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U064` | Corollary (5.10) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19696 | (5.10) Corollary. The completed zeta function $Z_{K}(s)$ admits an analytic continuation to C $\setminus\{0,1\}$ and satisfies the functional equation $$ Z _ {K} (s) = Z _ {K} (1 - s). $$ It has simple poles at $s = 0$ and $s = 1$ with residues $$ - \frac {2 ^ {r} h R}{w}, \quad r e s p. \frac {2 ^ {r} h R}{w}, $$ where $h$ is the class number of $K$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U065` | Corollary (5.11) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19762 | (5.11) Corollary. (i) The Dedekind zeta function $\zeta_K(s)$ has an analytic continuation to $\mathbb{C} \setminus \{1\}$ . (ii) At $s = 1$ it has a simple pole with residue $$ \kappa = \frac {2 ^ {r _ {1}} (2 \pi) ^ {r _ {2}}}{w \| d _ {K} \| ^ {1 / 2}} h R = h R / e ^ {g}. $$ Here h denotes the class number and $$ g = \log \frac {w \| d _ {K} \| ^ {1 / 2}}{2 ^ {r _ {1}} (2 \pi) ^ {r _ {2}}} $$ the genus of the number field $K$ (see chap. III, (3.5)). (iii) It satisfies the functional equation $$ \zeta_ {K} (1 - s) = A (s) \zeta_ {K} (s) $$ with the factor $$ A (s) = \left\| d _ {K} \right\| ^ {s - \frac {1}{2}} \left(\cos \frac {\pi s}{2}\right) ^ {r _ {1} + r _ {2}} \left(\sin \frac {\pi s}{2}\right) ^ {r _ {2}} L _ {\mathbb {C}} (s) ^ {n}. $$ | `FC09-C03-U058`, `FC09-C07-U044` |
| `FC09-C07-U066` | Proposition (5.12) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19802 | (5.12) Proposition. If $K = \mathbb{Q}(\mu_m)$ is the field of $m$ -th roots of unity, then $$ \zeta_ {K} (s) = G (s) \prod_ {\chi} L (\chi , s), $$ where $\chi$ varies over all Dirichlet characters mod m, and $$ G (s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \| m} (1 - \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {p}) ^ {- s}) ^ {- 1}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U067` | Proposition (5.13) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19842 | (5.13) Proposition. For every non-trivial Dirichlet character $\chi$ , one has $$ L (\chi , 1) \neq 0. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U068` | Dirichlet's Prime Number Theorem (5.14) | Chapter VII, §5, source L19850 | (5.14) Dirichlet's Prime Number Theorem. Every arithmetic progression $$ a, a \pm m, a \pm 2 m, a \pm 3 m, \dots , \text { with } (a, m) = 1, $$ i.e., every class a mod m, contains infinitely many prime numbers. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U069` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §6, source L19888 | Let $m$ be an integral ideal of the number field $K$ , and let $J^m$ be the group of all ideals of $K$ which are relatively prime to $m$ . Given any character $$ \chi : J ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \rightarrow S ^ {1} = \left\{z \in \mathbb {C} \mid \| z \| = 1 \right\}, $$ we may associate to it, as a common generalization of the Dirichlet L-series as well as the Dedekind zeta function, the L-series $$ L (\chi , s) = \sum_ {\mathfrak {a}} \frac {\chi (\mathfrak {a})}{\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {a}) ^ {s}}. $$ Here $\mathfrak{a}$ varies over all integral ideals of $K$ , and one defines $\chi(\mathfrak{a}) = 0$ whenever $(\mathfrak{a}, \mathfrak{m}) \neq 1$ . Searching for the most comprehensive class of characters $\chi$ for which the corresponding $L$ -series could be shown to have a functional equation, HECKE was led to the notion of Größencharaktere, which we define as follows. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U070` | Definition (6.1) | Chapter VII, §6, source L19902 | (6.1) Definition. A Größencharakter mod m is a character $\chi: J^{m} \to S^{1}$ for which there exists a pair of characters $$ \chi_ {\mathrm{f}}: (\mathcal {O} / \mathfrak {m}) ^ {*} \longrightarrow S ^ {1}, \quad \chi_ {\infty}: \mathbf {R} ^ {*} \longrightarrow S ^ {1}, $$ such that $$ \chi ((a)) = \chi_ {\mathrm{f}} (a) \chi_ {\infty} (a) $$ for every algebraic integer $a \in \mathcal{O}$ relatively prime to $\mathfrak{m}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U071` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §6, source L19928 | From $$ \mathcal {O} ^ {(\mathfrak {m})} = \left\{a \in \mathcal {O} \mid (a, \mathfrak {m}) = 1 \right\}, $$ it extends uniquely to the group $$ K ^ {(\mathfrak {m})} = \left\{a \in K ^ {*} \mid (a, \mathfrak {m}) = 1 \right\} $$ of all fractions relatively prime to m, because every $a \in K^{(m)}$ determines a well-defined class in $(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ . The character $\chi_{\infty}$ , and thus also the character $\chi_{f}$ , are determined uniquely by the Größencharakter $\chi$ , since the group $$ K ^ {\mathfrak {m}} = \left\{a \in K ^ {(\mathfrak {m})} \mid a \equiv 1 \bmod \mathfrak {m} \right\} $$ is dense in $\mathbf{R}^*$ , by the approximation theorem, and one has $\chi_{\infty}(a) = \chi((a))$ for $a \in K^{\mathfrak{m}}$ . Let us recall that the congruence $a \equiv 1 \mod \mathfrak{m}$ signifies that $a = b/c$ , for two integers $b, c$ relatively prime to $\mathfrak{m}$ , such that $b \equiv c \mod \mathfrak{m}$ or, equivalently, $a \in U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(n_{\mathfrak{p}})} \subseteq K_{\mathfrak{p}}$ for $\mathfrak{p}\|\mathfrak{m}$ , if $\mathfrak{m} = \prod_{\mathfrak{p}} \mathfrak{p}^{n_{\mathfrak{p}}}$ . The character $\chi_{\infty}$ factors automatically through $R^{*}/O^{m}$ , where $$ \mathcal {O} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} = \left\{\varepsilon \in \mathcal {O} ^ {*} \mid \varepsilon \equiv 1 \bmod \mathfrak {m} \right\}. $$ In fact, for $\varepsilon \in \mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ we have $\chi_{\mathrm{f}}(\varepsilon) = 1$ , and thus $\chi_{\infty}(\varepsilon) = \chi_{\mathrm{f}}(\varepsilon)\chi_{\infty}(\varepsilon) = \chi((\varepsilon)) = 1$ . The two characters $\chi_{\mathrm{f}}$ and $\chi_{\infty}$ of $(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ , resp. $\mathbf{R}^{*}/\mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ , associated with a Größencharakter $\chi$ satisfy the relation $$ \chi_ {\mathrm{f}} (\varepsilon) \chi_ {\infty} (\varepsilon) = 1 \quad \text { for all } \varepsilon \in \mathcal {O} ^ {*}, $$ and it can be shown that every such pair of characters $(\chi_{\mathrm{f}}, \chi_{\infty})$ comes from a Größencharakter $\chi$ (exercise 5). | — |
| `FC09-C07-U072` | Proposition (6.2) | Chapter VII, §6, source L19964 | (6.2) Proposition. Let $\chi$ be a Größencharakter mod $\mathfrak{m}$ , and let $\mathfrak{m}'$ be a divisor of $\mathfrak{m}$ . Then the following conditions are equivalent. (i) $\chi$ is the restriction of a Größencharakter $\chi': J^{\mathfrak{m}'} \to S^1 \bmod \mathfrak{m}'$ . (ii) $\chi_{\mathrm{f}}$ factors through $(\mathcal{O} / \mathfrak{m}')^{*}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U073` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §6, source L20004 | The Größencharakter $\chi \mod m$ is called primitive if it is not the restriction of a Größencharakter $\chi' \mod m'$ for any proper divisor $m'\|m$ . According to (6.2), this is the case if and only if the character $\chi_{f}$ of $(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ is primitive in the sense that it does not factorize through $(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m}')^{*}$ for any proper divisor $m'\|m$ . The conductor of $\chi$ is the smallest divisor f of m such that $\chi$ is the restriction of a Größencharakter mod f. By (6.2), f is the conductor of $\chi_{f}$ , i.e., the smallest divisor of m such that $\chi_{f}$ factors through $(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{f})^{*}$ . | `FC09-C07-U072` |
| `FC09-C07-U074` | Definition (6.3) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20010 | (6.3) Definition. Let $\chi_{\mathrm{f}}$ be a character of $(\mathcal{O} / \mathfrak{m})^{*}$ and $y\in \mathfrak{m}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}$ , where $\mathfrak{d}$ is the different of $K\|\mathbb{Q}$ . Then we define the Gauss sum of $\chi_{\mathrm{f}}$ to be $$ \tau_{\mathfrak{m}}(\chi_{\mathrm{f}},y) = \sum_{\substack{x\bmod \mathfrak{m}\\ (x,\mathfrak{m}) = 1}}\chi_{\mathrm{f}}(x)e^{2\pi i Tr(xy)}, $$ where $x$ varies over a system of representatives of $(\mathcal{O} / \mathfrak{m})^{*}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U075` | Theorem (6.4) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20026 | (6.4) Theorem. Let $\chi_{\mathrm{f}}$ be a primitive character of $(\mathcal{O} / \mathfrak{m})^{*}$ , let $y \in \mathfrak{m}^{-1}\mathfrak{d}^{-1}$ and $a \in \mathcal{O}$ . Then one has $$ \tau_ {\mathfrak {m}} (\chi_ {\mathrm{f}}, a y) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \overline {{\chi}} _ {\mathrm{f}} (a) \tau_ {\mathfrak {m}} (\chi_ {\mathrm{f}}, y), & i f (a, \mathfrak {m}) = 1, \\ 0, & i f (a, \mathfrak {m}) \neq 1, \end{array} \right. $$ and furthermore $$ \left\| \tau_ {\mathfrak {m}} (\chi_ {\mathrm{f}}, y) \right\| = \sqrt {\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {m})}, \quad i f (y \mathfrak {m} \mathfrak {d}, \mathfrak {m}) = 1. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U076` | Proposition (6.5) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20046 | (6.5) Proposition. If $\mathfrak{a} \neq 1$ , then $\sum_{\mathfrak{b} \mid \mathfrak{a}} \mu(\mathfrak{b}) = 0$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U077` | Lemma (6.6) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20062 | (6.6) Lemma. One has $$ T _ {1} (y) = \sum_ {\mathfrak {a} \| \mathfrak {m}} \mu (\mathfrak {a}) S _ {\mathfrak {a}} (y), $$ and for every divisor $\mathfrak{a}\|\mathfrak{m}$ , $$ S _ {\mathfrak {a}} (y) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \mathfrak {N} (\frac {\mathfrak {m}}{\mathfrak {a}}), & i f y \in \mathfrak {a} ^ {- 1} \mathfrak {d} ^ {- 1}, \\ 0, & i f y \notin \mathfrak {a} ^ {- 1} \mathfrak {d} ^ {- 1}. \end{array} \right. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U078` | Proposition (6.7) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20136 | (6.7) Proposition. The characters $\lambda$ of $\mathbf{R}^*$ , i.e., the continuous homomorphisms $$ \lambda : \mathbf {R} ^ {*} \longrightarrow S ^ {1}, $$ are given explicitly by $$ \lambda (x) = N \left(x ^ {p} \| x \| ^ {- p + i q}\right), $$ for some admissible $p \in \prod_{\tau} \mathbb{Z}$ (see §3, p.448) and a $q \in \mathbf{R}_{\pm}$ . $p$ and $q$ are uniquely determined by $\lambda$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U079` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §6, source L20196 | If the character $\chi_{\infty}$ associated to the Größencharakter $\chi: J^{\mathfrak{m}} \to S^{1}$ is given by $$ \chi_ {\infty} (x) = N \left(x ^ {p} \| x \| ^ {- p + i q}\right), $$ then we say that $\chi$ is of type $(p,q)$ , and we call $p - iq$ the exponent of $\chi$ . Since $\chi_{\infty}$ factors through $\mathbf{R}^{*} / \mathcal{O}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ , not all exponents actually occur (see exercise 3). | — |
| `FC09-C07-U080` | Definition (6.8) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20218 | (6.8) Definition. A Dirichlet character mod m is a character $$ \chi : J ^ {\mathfrak {m}} / P ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \longrightarrow S ^ {1} $$ of the ray class group mod m, i.e., a character $\chi : J^{\mathfrak{m}} \to S^{1}$ such that $\chi(P^{\mathfrak{m}}) = 1$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U081` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §6, source L20226 | The conductor of a Dirichlet character $\chi$ mod $\mathfrak{m}$ is defined to be the smallest module $f$ dividing $\mathfrak{m}$ such that $\chi$ factors through $J^{\mathfrak{f}} / P^{\mathfrak{f}}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U082` | Proposition (6.9) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20228 | (6.9) Proposition. The Dirichlet characters $\chi \mod m$ are precisely the Größencharaktere mod m of type $(p,0)$ , $p = (p_{\tau})$ , such that $p_{\tau} = 0$ for all complex $\tau$ . In other words, one has $$ \chi ((a)) = \chi_ {\mathrm{f}} (a) N \biggl (\left(\frac {a}{\| a \|}\right) ^ {p} \biggr), $$ for some character $\chi_{f}$ of $(\mathcal{O}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ . The conductor of the Dirichlet character is at the same time also the conductor of the corresponding Größencharakter. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U083` | Corollary (6.10) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20260 | (6.10) Corollary. The characters of the ideal class group $Cl_{K} = J/P$ , i.e., the characters $\chi : J \to S^{1}$ such that $\chi(P) = 1$ , are precisely the Größencharaktere $\chi \mod 1$ satisfying $\chi_{\infty} = 1$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U084` | Definition (6.11) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20272 | (6.11) Definition. A Hecke character is a character of the idèle class group $C = I / K^{*}$ of the number field $K$ , i.e., a continuous homomorphism $$ \chi : I \longrightarrow S ^ {1} $$ of the idèle group $I = \prod_{\mathfrak{p}} K_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}$ such that $\chi(K^{*}) = 1$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U085` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §6, source L20280 | In order to deal with Hecke characters concretely, consider an integral ideal $m = \prod_{p} p^{n_{p}}$ of K, i.e., $n_{p} \geq 0$ and $n_{p} = 0$ for $p \mid \infty$ . We associate to this ideal the subgroup $\bar{I}^{m}$ of I, $$ \bar {I} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} = I _ {\mathrm{f}} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \times I _ {\infty} \quad \text { where } \quad I _ {\mathrm{f}} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \nmid \infty} U _ {\mathfrak {p}} ^ {(n _ {\mathfrak {p}})}, \quad I _ {\infty} = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \| \infty} K _ {\mathfrak {p}} ^ {*}. $$ If $\mathfrak{p} \nmid \infty$ , then $U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(n)}$ is the group of units $U_{\mathfrak{p}}$ if $n = 0$ , and the $n$ -th group of higher units for $n \geq 1$ . We interpret $I_{\infty}$ as the multiplicative group $\mathbf{R}^*$ of the $\mathbb{R}$ -algebra $\mathbf{R} = K \otimes_{\mathbb{Q}} \mathbb{R} = \prod_{\mathfrak{p} \| \infty} K_{\mathfrak{p}}$ . Observe that $\bar{I}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ differs slightly from the congruence subgroup $I^{\mathfrak{m}} = \prod_{\mathfrak{p}} U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(n_{\mathfrak{p}})}$ introduced in chap. VI, §1, in that, for real $\mathfrak{p}$ , we have the factor $U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(0)} = \mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}$ instead of the component $K_{\mathfrak{p}}^{*}$ . The effect is that $I / \bar{I}^{\mathfrak{m}} K^{*}$ is not the ray class group $J^{\mathfrak{m}} / P^{\mathfrak{m}} \bmod \mathfrak{m}$ , but isomorphic to the quotient $J^{\mathfrak{m}} / \bar{P}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ by the group $\bar{P}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ of all principal ideals ( $a$ ) such that $a \equiv 1 \bmod \mathfrak{m}$ - this is seen as in chap. VI, (1.9). We will refer to $J^{\mathfrak{m}} / \bar{P}^{\mathfrak{m}}$ as the small ray class group. | `FC09-C06-U016`, `FC09-C07-U013` |
| `FC09-C07-U086` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §6, source L20288 | We call m a module of definition for the Hecke character $\chi$ if $$ \chi (I _ {\mathrm{f}} ^ {\mathfrak {m}}) = 1. $$ Every Hecke character admits a module of definition, since the image of $\chi : \prod_{\mathfrak{p} \nmid \infty} U_{\mathfrak{p}} \to S^1$ is a compact and totally disconnected subgroup of $S^1$ , hence finite, and so the kernel has to contain a subgroup of the form $\prod_{\mathfrak{p} \nmid \infty} U_{\mathfrak{p}}^{(n_{\mathfrak{p}})}$ where $n_{\mathfrak{p}} = 0$ for almost all $\mathfrak{p}$ . For it we can take the ideal $\mathfrak{m} = \prod_{\mathfrak{p} \nmid \infty} \mathfrak{p}^{n_{\mathfrak{p}}}$ as a module of definition. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U087` | Proposition (6.12) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20312 | (6.12) Proposition. There is an exact sequence $$ 1 \longrightarrow \mathbf {R} ^ {*} / \mathcal {O} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \longrightarrow C (\mathfrak {m}) \longrightarrow J ^ {\mathfrak {m}} / \bar {P} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \longrightarrow 1. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U088` | Proposition (6.13) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20344 | (6.13) Proposition. There is a canonical exact sequence $$ 1 \longrightarrow K ^ {(\mathfrak {m})} / \mathcal {O} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \stackrel {\delta} {\longrightarrow} J ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \times (\mathcal {O} / \mathfrak {m}) ^ {*} \times \mathbf {R} ^ {*} / \mathcal {O} ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \stackrel {f} {\longrightarrow} C (\mathfrak {m}) \longrightarrow 1, $$ where $\delta$ is given by $$ \delta (a) = \left((a) ^ {- 1}, a \bmod \mathfrak {m}, a \bmod \mathcal {O} ^ {\mathfrak {m}}\right). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U089` | Corollary (6.14) | Chapter VII, §6, source L20450 | (6.14) Corollary. The correspondence $\chi \mapsto \chi \circ c$ is $1 - 1$ between characters $\chi$ of $C(\mathfrak{m})$ , i.e., Hecke characters with module of definition $\mathfrak{m}$ , and Größencharaktere mod $\mathfrak{m}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U090` | Proposition (7.1) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20506 | (7.1) Proposition. There is a commutative exact diagram ![](images/1165cfa4f657633d67f7df5beebfabe64b8c2f77bdf30fa1660304ae211b12e6.jpg) with a subgroup $\widehat{K}^* \subseteq \mathbf{C}^*$ containing $K^*$ such that $\|a\| \in \mathbf{R}_+^*$ , and $$ \mathfrak {N} \bigl ((a) \bigr) = \left\| N (a) \right\| $$ for all $a \in \widehat{K}^*$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U091` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §7, source L20518 | Let the ideal class group $J / P$ be given by a basis $[\mathfrak{b}_1], \ldots, [\mathfrak{b}_r]$ , and choose, for every one of these basic classes, an ideal $\mathfrak{b}_1, \ldots, \mathfrak{b}_r$ . Then every fractional ideal $\mathfrak{a} \in J$ can be written in the form $$ \mathfrak {a} = a \mathfrak {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1}} \dots \mathfrak {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r}} $$ where $a \in K^{*}$ is well-determined up to a unit $\varepsilon \in \mathcal{O}^{*}$ , and the exponents $\nu_{i} \mod h_{i}$ are uniquely determined, $h_{i}$ being the order of $[\mathfrak{b}_i]$ in $J/P$ . Let $\mathfrak{b}_i^{h_i} = (b_i)$ . For every $\tau \in \operatorname{Hom}(K, \mathbb{C})$ , we choose a fixed root $$ \widehat {b} _ {i \tau} = \sqrt [ h _ {i} ]{\tau b _ {i}} $$ in $\mathbb{C}$ in such a way that $\widehat{b}_{i\overline{\tau}} = \widehat{b}_{i\tau}$ whenever $\tau$ is complex. We define $\widehat{K}^*$ to be the subgroup of $\mathbf{C}^*$ generated by $K^*$ and by the elements $\widehat{b}_i = (\widehat{b}_{i\tau})\in \mathbf{C}^*$ . Each class $[\mathfrak{b}]\in J / P$ contains a uniquely determined ideal of the form $$ \mathfrak {b} = \mathfrak {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1}} \dots \mathfrak {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r}} \quad \text { with } \quad 0 \leq \nu_ {i} < h _ {i}, $$ and we consider the mapping $$ f: J / P \longrightarrow \widehat {K} ^ {*} / K ^ {*}, \quad f ([ \mathfrak {b} ]) = \widehat {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1}} \dots \widehat {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r}} \bmod K ^ {*}. $$ It is a homomorphism, for if $\mathfrak{b} = \mathfrak{b}_1^{\nu_1}\dots \mathfrak{b}_r^{\nu_r}$ and $\mathfrak{b}' = \mathfrak{b}_1^{\nu_1'}\dots \mathfrak{b}_r^{\nu_r'}$ , and if $\nu_{i} + \nu_{i}' = \mu_{i} + \lambda_{i}h_{i}, 0\leq \mu_{i} < h_{i}$ , then $\mathfrak{b}_1^{\mu_1}\dots \mathfrak{b}_r^{\mu_r}$ is the ideal belonging to the class $[\mathfrak{b}][\mathfrak{b}']$ , and $$ \begin{array}{c} f ([ \mathfrak {b} ] [ \mathfrak {b} ^ {\prime} ]) = \widehat {b} _ {1} ^ {\mu_ {1}} \dots \widehat {b} _ {r} ^ {\mu_ {r}} \equiv \widehat {b} _ {1} ^ {\mu_ {1}} \dots \widehat {b} _ {r} ^ {\mu_ {r}} b _ {1} ^ {\lambda_ {1}} \dots b _ {r} ^ {\lambda_ {r}} \\ \equiv (\widehat {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1}} \dots \widehat {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r}}) (\widehat {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1} ^ {\prime}} \dots \widehat {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r} ^ {\prime}}) \text {mod} K ^ {*} = f ([ \mathfrak {b} ]) f ([ \mathfrak {b} ^ {\prime} ]). \end{array} $$ f is clearly surjective. To show the injectivity, let $\widehat{b}_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\cdots\widehat{b}_{r}^{\nu_{r}}=a\in K^{*}$ , and let $h=h_{1}\cdots h_{r}$ be the class number of K. Then we have for the ideal $a = a^{-1}b_{1}^{\nu_{1}} \cdots b_{r}^{\nu_{r}} \in J$ that $a^{h} = a^{-h}(b_{1}^{\nu_{1}h/h_{1}} \cdots b_{r}^{\nu_{r}h/h_{r}}) = a^{-h}(\widehat{b}_{1}^{\nu_{1}} \cdots \widehat{b}_{r}^{\nu_{r}})^{h} = (1)$ . Since J is torsion-free, it follows that $a = (1)$ , and so $b_{1}^{\nu_{1}} \cdots b_{r}^{\nu_{r}} = (a) \in P$ . From this we deduce that every element $\widehat{a} \in \widehat{K}^{*}$ admits a unique representation | — |
| `FC09-C07-U092` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §7, source L20554 | We define a map $$ (\quad): \widehat {K} ^ {*} \longrightarrow J $$ by $$ \widehat {a} = a \widehat {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1}} \dots \widehat {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r}} \longmapsto (\widehat {a}) = a \mathfrak {b} _ {1} ^ {\nu_ {1}} \dots \mathfrak {b} _ {r} ^ {\nu_ {r}}. $$ Arguing as above, we see that this is a homomorphism. It is surjective and obviously has kernel $\mathcal{O}^*$ . Finally we have that $\|\widehat{b_i}\| = (\|\widehat{b_{i\tau}}\|) \in \mathbf{R}_+^*$ and $$ \mathfrak {N} \big ((\widehat {b _ {i}}) \big) ^ {h _ {i}} = \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {b} _ {i} ^ {h _ {i}}) = \left\| N (b _ {i}) \right\| = \left\| \prod_ {\tau} \tau b _ {i} \right\| = \left\| \prod_ {\tau} \widehat {b} _ {i \tau} ^ {h _ {i}} \right\| = \left\| N (\widehat {b _ {i}}) \right\| ^ {h _ {i}}, $$ so that $\mathfrak{N}((\widehat{b}_i)) = \|N(\widehat{b}_i)\|$ , and thus $\|a\| \in \mathbf{R}_+^*$ , $\mathfrak{N}((a)) = \|N(a)\|$ for all $a \in \widehat{K}^*$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U093` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §7, source L20574 | The elements $a$ of $\widehat{K}^*$ used to be called ideal numbers - a name which is somewhat forgotten but will be used in what follows. The diagram (7.1) implies an isomorphism $$ \widehat {K} ^ {*} / K ^ {*} \cong J / P. $$ For $a, b \in \widehat{K}^*$ we write $a \sim b$ if $a$ and $b$ lie in the same class, i.e., if $ab^{-1} \in K^*$ . We call $a$ an ideal integer, or an integral ideal number, if (a) is an integral ideal. The semigroup of all ideal integers will be denoted by $\widehat{\mathcal{O}}$ . Furthermore we write $a \mid b$ if $\frac{b}{a} \in \widehat{\mathcal{O}}$ , and for every pair $a, b \in \widehat{K}^*$ , we have the notion of $\gcd(a, b) \in \widehat{K}^*$ (which is lacking inside $K^*$ ). The greatest common divisor is the ideal number $d$ (which is unique up to a unit) such that the ideal (d) is the gcd of the ideals (a), (b). Observe that the ideal numbers are not defined in a canonical way. This is the reason why they have not been able to hold their own in the development of number theory. (They are treated in [46], [65].) | `FC09-C07-U090` |
| `FC09-C07-U094` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §7, source L20582 | We now form an analogous extension of the prime residue groups $(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z})^{*}$ . For three ideal numbers a, b, m, the congruence $$ a \equiv b \bmod m $$ signifies that $a \sim b$ and $\frac{a-b}{m} \in \widehat{\mathcal{O}} \cup \{0\}$ . If $\mathfrak{m} = (m)$ , we also write this relation as $a \equiv b \mod \mathfrak{m}$ . Let $\mathfrak{m}$ be an integral ideal. The semigroup $\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(\mathfrak{m})}$ of all integral ideal numbers relatively prime to $\mathfrak{m}$ is partitioned by the equivalence relation $\equiv$ into classes, which we will write as $a \mod \mathfrak{m}$ . They are given explicitly as follows. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U095` | Lemma (7.2) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20590 | (7.2) Lemma. For every $a \in \widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(\mathfrak{m})}$ one has $$ a \bmod {\mathfrak {m}} = a + a (a ^ {- 1}) {\mathfrak {m}}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U096` | Proposition (7.3) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20612 | (7.3) Proposition. $(\widehat{\mathcal{O}} / \mathfrak{m})^{*}$ is an abelian group, and we have a canonical exact sequence $$ 1 \longrightarrow (\mathcal {O} / \mathfrak {m}) ^ {*} \longrightarrow (\widehat {\mathcal {O}} / \mathfrak {m}) ^ {*} \longrightarrow J / P \longrightarrow 1. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U097` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §7, source L20622 | For an ideal class $\mathcal{K} \in J / P$ , we will denote by $\mathcal{K}' \in J / P$ in what follows the class defined by $$ \mathcal {K} \mathcal {K} ^ {\prime} = [ \mathfrak {m d} ], $$ where $\mathfrak{d}$ is the different of $K\|\mathbb{Q}$ . Let $\mathfrak{m} = (m)$ and $\mathfrak{d} = (d)$ , with some fixed ideal numbers $m, d$ . For $\mathfrak{m} = \mathcal{O}$ let $m = 1$ . We now study characters $$ \chi : (\widehat {\mathcal {O}} / \mathfrak {m}) ^ {*} \longrightarrow \mathbb {C} ^ {*}, $$ and put $\chi(a)=0$ for $a\in\mathcal{O}$ such that $(a,\mathfrak{m})\neq1$ . In the applications, $\chi$ will come from a Größencharakter mod m, but the treatment of the theta series is independent of such an origin of $\chi$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U098` | Definition (7.4) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20636 | (7.4) Definition. Let $a \in \widehat{\mathcal{O}}$ be an ideal integer, and let $\mathcal{K}$ be the class of $(a)$ . Then we define the Gauss sum $$ \tau (\chi , a) = \sum_ {\hat {x} \bmod m} \chi (\widehat {x}) e ^ {2 \pi i T r (\hat {x} a / m d)}, $$ where $\widehat{x}$ mod m runs through the classes of $(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ which are mapped to the class $K'$ . In particular, we put $\tau(\chi)=\tau(\chi,1)$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U099` | Proposition (7.5) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20676 | (7.5) Proposition. For a primitive character $\chi$ of $(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ , one has $$ \tau (\chi , a) = \overline {{\chi}} (a) \tau (\chi) $$ and $\|\tau (\chi)\| = \sqrt{\mathfrak{N}(\mathfrak{m})}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U100` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §7, source L20684 | The theta series $\theta(\chi, z)$ used in §2 in the treatment of Dirichlet $L$ -series are attached to the field $\mathbb{Q}$ . We now have to find their analogues relative to an arbitrary number field $K$ . Given any admissible element $p \in \prod_{\tau} \mathbb{Z}$ (see §3, p.448) and a character $\chi$ of $(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}/\mathfrak{m})^*$ , we form the Hecke theta series $$ \theta^ {p} (\chi , z) = \sum_ {a \in \hat {\mathcal {O}} \cup \{0 \}} \chi (a) N (a ^ {p}) e ^ {\pi i \langle a z / \| m d \|, a \rangle}, $$ where m, d are fixed ideal numbers such that $(m) = \mathfrak{m}$ and $(d) = \mathfrak{d}$ . We take m = 1 if $\mathfrak{m} = 1$ . The case $\mathfrak{m} = 1$ , p = 0 is exceptional in that the constant term of the theta series is $\chi(0)N(0^{p}) = 1$ , whereas it is 0 in all other cases. Let us decompose the theta series according to the ideal classes $\mathfrak{K} \in J / P$ into partial Hecke theta series $$ \theta^ {p} (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , z) = \sum_ {a \in (\hat {\mathfrak {K}} \cap \hat {\mathcal {O}}) \cup \{0 \}} \chi (a) N (a ^ {p}) e ^ {\pi i \langle a z / \| m d \|, a \rangle}, $$ where $a$ varies over all ideal integers in the class $\widehat{\mathfrak{K}}\in\widehat{K}^{*}/K^{*}$ which corresponds to the ideal class $\mathfrak{K}$ under the isomorphism $\widehat{K}^{*}/K^{*}\cong J/P$ . For these partial theta series, we want to deduce a transformation formula, and to this end we decompose them further into theta series for which we have the general transformation formula (3.6) at our disposal. | `FC09-C07-U045` |
| `FC09-C07-U101` | Lemma (7.6) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20702 | (7.6) Lemma. Assume that $\mathfrak{m} \neq 1$ or $p \neq 0$ . If $x \bmod \mathfrak{m}$ varies over the classes of $(\mathcal{O} / \mathfrak{m})^*$ , then one has $$ \theta^ {p} (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , z) = \chi (a) N (a ^ {p}) \sum_ {x \bmod \mathfrak {m}} \chi (x) \theta_ {\Gamma} ^ {p} \bigl (x, 0, z \| a ^ {2} / m d \| \bigr), $$ where $\Gamma$ is the lattice $\mathfrak{m} / \mathfrak{a} \subseteq \mathbf{R}$ and $$ \theta_ {\Gamma} ^ {p} (x, 0, z) = \sum_ {g \in \Gamma} N \big ((x + g) ^ {p} \big) e ^ {\pi i \langle (x + g) z, x + g \rangle}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U102` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §7, source L20732 | For any admissible element $p = (p_{\tau})$ , we will write $\overline{p}$ for the admissible element with components $\overline{p}_{\tau} = p_{\overline{\tau}}$ . From the transformation formula (3.6) for the series $\theta_{\Gamma}^{p}$ and proposition (7.5) on Gauss sums, we now obtain the | `FC09-C07-U045`, `FC09-C07-U099` |
| `FC09-C07-U103` | Theorem (7.7) | Chapter VII, §7, source L20734 | (7.7) Theorem. For a primitive character $\chi$ of $(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}/\mathfrak{m})^{*}$ , one has the transformation formula $$ \theta^ {p} (\mathcal {K}, \chi , - 1 / z) = W (\chi , \overline {{p}}) N \big ((z / i) ^ {p + \frac {1}{2}} \big) \theta^ {\overline {{p}}} (\mathcal {K} ^ {\prime}, \overline {{\chi}}, z) $$ with the constant factor $$ W (\chi , \overline {{p}}) = \left[ i ^ {T r (\overline {{p}})} N \left(\left(\frac {m d}{\| m d \|}\right) ^ {\overline {{p}}}\right) \right] ^ {- 1} \frac {\tau (\chi)}{\sqrt {\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {m})}}. $$ This factor has absolute value $\|W(\chi, \overline{p})\| = 1$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U104` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §8, source L20888 | Let $\mathfrak{m}$ be again an integral ideal of the number field $K$ and let $$ \chi : J ^ {\mathfrak {m}} \longrightarrow S ^ {1} $$ be a character of the group of ideals relatively prime to m. With respect to this character, we form the L-series $$ L (\chi , s) = \sum_ {a} \frac {\chi (a)}{\mathfrak {N} (a) ^ {s}}, $$ where a varies over the integral ideals of K and we put $\chi(\mathfrak{a}) = 0$ whenever $(\mathfrak{a}, \mathfrak{m}) \neq 1$ . Then the following proposition holds in complete generality. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U105` | Proposition (8.1) | Chapter VII, §8, source L20902 | (8.1) Proposition. The L-series $L(\chi, s)$ converges absolutely and uniformly in the domain $\operatorname{Re}(s) \geq 1 + \delta$ , for all $\delta > 0$ , and one has $$ L (\chi , s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p}} \frac {1}{1 - \chi (\mathfrak {p}) \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {p}) ^ {- s}}, $$ where p varies over the prime ideals of K. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U106` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §8, source L20998 | The L-series of a Größencharacter of $J^{m}$ is called a Hecke L-series. If $\chi$ is a (generalized) Dirichlet character mod m, i.e., a character of the ray class group $J^{m}/P^{m}$ , then we call it a (generalized) Dirichlet L-series. The proof of the functional equation of the Hecke L-series proceeds in exactly the same way as for the Dedekind zeta function, except that it is based on the theta transformation formula (7.7). | `FC09-C07-U103` |
| `FC09-C07-U107` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §8, source L21032 | The character $\chi_{\infty}$ of $R^{*}$ corresponding to $\chi$ is given by (6.7) as $$ \chi_ {\infty} (x) = N \left(x ^ {p} \| x \| ^ {- p + i q}\right), $$ for an admissible $p \in \prod_{\tau} \mathbb{Z}$ and a $q \in \mathbf{R}_{\pm}$ . We put $\mathbf{s} = s\mathbf{1} + p - iq$ , where $s \in \mathbb{C}$ is a single complex variable, and $$ L _ {\infty} (\chi , s) = L _ {X} (\mathbf {s}) = L _ {X} (s \mathbf {1} + p - i q). $$ | `FC09-C07-U078` |
| `FC09-C07-U108` | Proposition (8.3) | Chapter VII, §8, source L21164 | (8.3) Proposition. The function $$ \Lambda (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , s) = \left(\| d _ {K} \| \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {m})\right) ^ {s / 2} L _ {\infty} (\chi , s) L (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , s) $$ is the Mellin transform $$ \varLambda (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , s) = L (f, s ^ {\prime}) $$ of the function $$ f (t) = f _ {F} (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , t) = \frac {c (\chi)}{w} \int_ {F} N (x ^ {(p - i q) / 2}) \theta (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , i x t ^ {1 / n}) d ^ {*} x $$ at $s' = \frac{1}{2}(s + Tr(p - iq) / n)$ . Here we have set $n = [K : \mathbb{Q}]$ , $c(\chi) = N(\|md\|^{-p + iq})^{1/2}$ , and $w$ denotes the number of roots of unity in $K$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U109` | Proposition (8.4) | Chapter VII, §8, source L21236 | (8.4) Proposition. We have $f_{F}(\mathfrak{K},\chi ,t) = a_{0} + O(e^{-ct^{1 / n}})$ for some $c > 0$ , and $$ a _ {0} = \frac {N (\| d \| ^ {i q / 2})}{w} \int_ {F} N (x ^ {- i q / 2}) d ^ {*} x $$ if $\mathfrak{m} = 1$ and $p = 0$ , and $a_0 = 0$ otherwise. Furthermore we have $$ f _ {F} \Big (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , \frac {1}{t} \Big) = W (\chi) t ^ {\frac {1}{2} + T r (p) / n} f _ {F ^ {- 1}} (\mathfrak {K} ^ {\prime}, \overline {{\chi}}, t) $$ where $\mathfrak{K}\mathfrak{K}' = [\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{d}]$ , and the constant factor is given by $$ W (\chi) = \left[ i ^ {T r (\overline {{p}})} N \left(\left(\frac {m d}{\| m d \|}\right) ^ {\overline {{p}}}\right) \right] ^ {- 1} \frac {\tau (\chi_ {\mathrm{f}})}{\sqrt {\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {m})}}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U110` | Theorem (8.5) | Chapter VII, §8, source L21274 | (8.5) Theorem. Let $\chi$ be a primitive Größencharacter mod $\mathfrak{m}$ . Then the function $$ \varLambda (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , s) = \left(\| d _ {K} \| \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {m})\right) ^ {s / 2} L _ {\infty} (\chi , s) L (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , s), \quad \mathrm{Re} (s) > 1, $$ has a meromorphic continuation to the complex plane $\mathbb{C}$ and satisfies the functional equation $$ \Lambda (\mathfrak {K}, \chi , s) = W (\chi) \Lambda (\mathfrak {K} ^ {\prime}, \overline {{{{\chi}}}}, 1 - s) $$ where $\mathfrak{K}\mathfrak{K}' = [\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{d}]$ , and the constant factor is given by $$ W (\chi) = \left[ i ^ {T r (\overline {{p}})} N \left(\left(\frac {m d}{\| m d \|}\right) ^ {\overline {{p}}}\right) \right] ^ {- 1} \frac {\tau (\chi_ {\mathrm{f}})}{\sqrt {\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {m})}}. $$ It has absolute value $\|W(\chi)\| = 1$ . $\Lambda(\mathfrak{K},\chi,s)$ is holomorphic except for poles of order at most one at $s=Tr(-p+iq)/n$ and $s=1+Tr(p+iq)/n$ . In the case $m\neq1$ or $p\neq0$ , $\Lambda(\mathfrak{K},\chi,s)$ is holomorphic on all of C. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U111` | Corollary (8.6) | Chapter VII, §8, source L21320 | (8.6) Corollary. The $L$ -series $\Lambda(\chi, s)$ admits a holomorphic continuation to $$ \mathbb {C} \setminus \left\{T r (- p + i q) / n, 1 + T r (p + i q) / n \right\} $$ and satisfies the functional equation $$ \Lambda (\chi , s) = W (\chi) \Lambda (\overline {{{{\chi}}}}, 1 - s). $$ It is holomorphic on all of $\mathbb{C}$ , if $\mathfrak{m} \neq 1$ or $p \neq 0$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U112` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §9, source L21346 | We first prove a new kind of unit theorem for which we need the following notions from linear algebra. Let V be an n-dimensional R-vector space, k a subfield of R, and $V_{k}$ a fixed k-structure of V, i.e., a k-subspace such that $V = V_{k} \otimes_{k} R$ . By definition, an (open) k-rational simplicial cone of dimension d is a subset of the form $$ C \left(v _ {1}, \dots , v _ {d}\right) = \left\{t _ {1} v _ {1} + \dots + t _ {d} v _ {d} \mid t _ {\ell} \in \mathbb {R} _ {+} ^ {*} \right\} $$ where $v_{1}, \ldots, v_{d}$ are linearly independent vectors in $V_{k}$ . A finite disjoint union of k-rational simplicial cones is called a k-rational polyhedric cone. We call a linear form L on V k-rational if its coefficients with respect to a k-basis of $V_{k}$ lie in k. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U113` | Lemma (9.1) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21354 | (9.1) Lemma. Every nonempty subset different from $\{0\}$ of the form $$ P = \left\{x \in V \mid L _ {i} (x) \geq 0, \quad 0 < i \leq \ell , M _ {j} (x) > 0, 0 < j \leq m \right\} $$ with nonzero k-rational linear forms $L_{i}$ , $M_{j}$ ( $\ell = 0$ or m = 0 is allowed) is a disjoint union of finitely many k-rational cones, and possibly the origin. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U114` | Corollary (9.2) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21390 | (9.2) Corollary. If $C$ and $C'$ are $k$ -rational polyhedric cones, then $C \setminus C'$ is also a $k$ -rational polyhedric cone. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U115` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §9, source L21412 | Let $K$ be a number field of degree $n = [K : \mathbb{Q}]$ , and let $\mathbf{R} = \left[\prod_{\tau} \mathbb{C}\right]^+$ be the corresponding Minkowski space $(\tau \in \operatorname{Hom}(K, \mathbb{C}))$ . Define $$ \mathbf {R} _ {(+)} ^ {*} = \left\{\left(x _ {\tau}\right) \in \mathbf {R} ^ {*} \mid x _ {\tau} > 0 \text { for all real } \tau \right\}. $$ (Observe that one has $\mathbf{R}_{(+)}^{*} = \mathbf{R}_{+}^{*}$ only in the case where $K$ is totally real.) Since $\mathbf{R} = K\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}$ , the field $K$ is a $\mathbb{Q}$ -structure of $\mathbf{R}$ . The group $$ \mathcal {O} _ {+} ^ {*} = \mathcal {O} ^ {*} \cap \mathbf {R} _ {(+)} ^ {*} $$ of totally positive units acts on $\mathbf{R}_{(+)}^{*}$ via multiplication, and we will show that this action has a fundamental domain which is a Q-rational polyhedric cone: | — |
| `FC09-C07-U116` | Shintani's Unit Theorem (9.3) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21426 | (9.3) Shintani's Unit Theorem. If $E$ is a subgroup of finite index in $\mathcal{O}_+^*$ , then there exists a $\mathbb{Q}$ -rational polyhedric cone $P$ such that $$ \mathbf {R} _ {(+)} ^ {*} = \bigcup_ {\varepsilon \in E} \varepsilon P \quad (d i s j o i n t u n i o n). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U117` | Lemma (9.4) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21532 | (9.4) Lemma. There is a bijection $$ (1 + \mathfrak {a} ^ {- 1} \mathfrak {m}) _ {+} / E \stackrel {{\sim}} {{\longrightarrow}} \mathfrak {K} _ {\text { int }}, \quad \bar {a} \longmapsto a \mathfrak {a}, $$ onto the set $\mathcal{K}_{\mathrm{int}}$ of integral ideals in $\mathcal{K}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U118` | Proposition (9.5) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21574 | (9.5) Proposition. The sets $R(\mathfrak{K}, C_i)$ are finite, and one has $$ \zeta (\mathfrak {K}, s) = \frac {1}{\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {a}) ^ {s}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \sum_ {x \in R (\mathfrak {K}, C _ {i})} \zeta (C _ {i}, x, s) $$ with the zeta functions $$ \zeta (C _ {i}, x, s) = \sum_ {\mathbf {z}} \left\| N (x + z _ {1} v _ {i 1} + \dots + z _ {d _ {i}} v _ {i d _ {i}}) \right\| ^ {- s}, $$ where $\mathbf{z} = (z_{1},\dots ,z_{d_{i}})$ varies over all $d_{i}$ -tuples of nonnegative integers. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U119` | Corollary (9.6) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21618 | (9.6) Corollary. For the Dirichlet $L$ -series attached to the Dirichlet character $\chi : J^{\mathfrak{m}} / P^{\mathfrak{m}} \to \mathbb{C}^{*}$ , we have the decomposition $$ L (\chi , s) = \sum_ {\mathfrak {K}} \frac {\chi (\mathfrak {a})}{\mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {a}) ^ {s}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \sum_ {x \in R (\mathfrak {K}, C _ {i})} \zeta (C _ {i}, x, s), $$ where $\mathfrak{K}$ runs through the classes $J^{\mathfrak{m}} / P^{\mathfrak{m}}$ , and $\mathfrak{a}$ denotes an integral ideal in $\mathfrak{K}$ , one for each class. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U120` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §9, source L21628 | Let $A$ be a real $r \times n$ -matrix, $r \leq n$ , with positive entries $a_{ji}$ , $1 \leq j \leq r$ , $1 \leq i \leq n$ . From this matrix we construct the linear forms $$ L _ {j} (t _ {1}, \dots , t _ {n}) = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} a _ {j i} t _ {i} \quad \text { and } \quad L _ {i} ^ {*} (z _ {1}, \dots , z _ {r}) = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {r} a _ {j i} z _ {j}. $$ For an $r$ -tuple $x = (x_1, \ldots, x_r)$ of positive real numbers, we write the following series $$ \zeta (A, x, s) = \sum_ {z _ {1}, \dots , z _ {r} = 0} ^ {\infty} \prod_ {i = 1} ^ {n} L _ {i} ^ {*} (z + x) ^ {- s}. $$ On the other hand we define the generalized Bernoulli polynomials $B_{k}(A,x)$ by $$ B _ {k} (A, x) = \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} B _ {k} (A, x) ^ {(i)}, $$ where $B_{k}(A,x)^{(i)} / (k!)^{n}$ is the coefficient of $$ u ^ {(k - 1) n} (t _ {1} \dots t _ {i - 1} t _ {i + 1} \dots t _ {n}) ^ {k - 1} $$ in the Laurent expansion at 0 of the function $$ \prod_ {j = 1} ^ {r} \left. \frac {\exp (u x _ {j} L _ {j} (t))}{\exp (u L _ {j} (t)) - 1} \right\| _ {t _ {i} = 1} $$ in the variables $u, t_1, \ldots, t_{i-1}, t_{i+1}, \ldots, t_n$ . For $r = n = 1$ and $A = a$ , we have $B_k(a, x) = a^{k-1}B_k(x)$ , with the usual Bernoulli polynomial $B_k(x)$ (see §1, exercise 2). The equation $$ B _ {k} (A, 1 - x) = (- 1) ^ {n (k - 1) + r} B _ {k} (A, x), $$ where $1 - x$ signifies $(1 - x_{1},\ldots ,1 - x_{r})$ , is easily proved. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U121` | Proposition (9.7) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21666 | (9.7) Proposition. The series $\zeta(A, x, s)$ is absolutely convergent for $\operatorname{Re}(s) > r/n$ , and it can be meromorphically continued to the whole complex plane. Its values at the points $s = 1 - k$ , $k = 1, 2, \ldots$ , are given by $$ \zeta (A, x, 1 - k) = (- 1) ^ {r} \frac {B _ {k} (A , x)}{k ^ {n}}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U122` | Theorem (9.8) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21798 | (9.8) Theorem. The values of the partial zeta function $\zeta(\mathfrak{K}, s)$ at the integral points $s = 1 - k$ , $k = 1, 2, 3, \ldots$ , are given by $$ \zeta (\mathfrak {K}, 1 - k) = \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {a}) ^ {k - 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left[ (- 1) ^ {d _ {i}} \sum_ {x \in R (\mathfrak {K}, C _ {i})} \frac {B _ {k} (A _ {i} , x)}{k ^ {n}} \right], $$ and the values of the Dirichlet $L$ -series $L(\chi, s)$ are given by $$ L (\chi , 1 - k) = \sum_ {\mathfrak {K}} \chi (\mathfrak {a}) \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {a}) ^ {k - 1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {m} \left[ (- 1) ^ {d _ {i}} \sum_ {x \in R (\mathfrak {K}, C _ {i})} \frac {B _ {k} (A _ {i} , x)}{k ^ {n}} \right]. $$ Here $\mathfrak{a}$ is an integral ideal in the class $\mathfrak{K}$ of $J^{\mathfrak{m}} / P^{\mathfrak{m}}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U123` | Corollary (9.9) | Chapter VII, §9, source L21820 | (9.9) Corollary (SIEGEL-KLINGEN). The values of the partial zeta function $\zeta(\mathcal{R}, s)$ at the points $s = 0, -1, -2, \ldots$ are rational numbers. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U124` | Definition (10.1) | Chapter VII, §10, source L21912 | (10.1) Definition. Let $L\|K$ be a Galois extension of algebraic number fields with Galois group $G$ , and let $(\rho, V)$ be a representation of $G$ . Then the Artin $L$ -series attached to $\rho$ is defined to be $$ \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \rho , s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p}} \frac {1}{\det (1 - \varphi_ {\mathfrak {P}} \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {p}) ^ {- s} ; V ^ {I _ {\mathfrak {P}}})}, $$ where $\mathfrak{p}$ runs through all prime ideals of $K$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U125` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §10, source L21936 | The degree of a representation $(\rho, V)$ of a finite group G is the dimension of V. The representation is called irreducible if the G-module V does not admit any proper G-invariant subspace. An irreducible representation of an abelian group is simply a character $$ \rho : G \longrightarrow \mathbb {C} ^ {*} = G L _ {1} (\mathbb {C}). $$ Two representations $(\rho, V)$ and $(\rho', V')$ are called equivalent if the G-modules V and $V'$ are isomorphic. Every representation $(\rho, V)$ factors into a direct sum $$ V = V _ {1} \oplus \dots \oplus V _ {s} $$ of irreducible representations. If an irreducible representation $(\rho_{\alpha}, V_{\alpha})$ is equivalent to precisely $r_{\alpha}$ among the representations in this decomposition, then $r_{\alpha}$ is called the multiplicity of $\rho_{\alpha}$ in $\rho$ , and one writes $$ \rho \sim \sum_ {\alpha} r _ {\alpha} \rho_ {\alpha}, $$ where $\rho_{\alpha}$ varies over all non-equivalent irreducible representations of G. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U126` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §10, source L21956 | The character of a representation $(\rho, V)$ is by definition the function $$ \chi_ {\rho}: G \longrightarrow \mathbb {C}, \quad \chi_ {\rho} (\sigma) = \operatorname{trace} \rho (\sigma). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U127` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §10, source L21962 | One has $\chi_{\rho}(1)=\dim V=\operatorname{degree}(\rho)$ , and $\chi_{\rho}(\sigma\tau\sigma^{-1})=\chi_{\rho}(\tau)$ for all $\sigma,\tau\in G$ . In general, a function $f:G\to C$ with the property that $f(\sigma\tau\sigma^{-1})=f(\tau)$ is called a central function (or class function). The special importance of characters comes from the following fact: Two representations are equivalent if and only if their characters are equal. If $\rho \sim \sum_{\alpha} r_{\alpha} \rho_{\alpha}$ , then $$ \chi_ {\rho} = \sum_ {\alpha} r _ {\alpha} \chi_ {\rho_ {\alpha}}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U128` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §10, source L21970 | The character of the trivial representation $\rho: G \to GL(V)$ , $\dim V = 1$ , $\rho(\sigma) = 1$ for all $\sigma \in G$ , is the constant function of value 1, and is denoted by $1_{G}$ , or simply 1. The regular representation is given by the G-module $$ V = \mathbb {C} [ G ] = \left\{\sum_ {\tau \in G} x _ {\tau} \tau \mid x _ {\tau} \in \mathbb {C} \right\}, $$ on which the $\sigma\in G$ act via multiplication on the left. It decomposes into the direct sum of the trivial representation $V_{0}=\mathbb{C}\sum_{\sigma\in G}\sigma$ , and the augmentation representation $\left\{\sum_{\sigma\in G}x_{\sigma}\sigma\mid\sum_{\sigma}x_{\sigma}=0\right\}$ . The character associated with the regular, resp. the augmentation representation, is denoted by $r_{G}$ , resp. $u_{G}$ . We thus have $r_{G}=u_{G}+1_{G}$ , and explicitly: $r_{G}(\sigma)=0$ for $\sigma\neq1$ , $r_{G}(1)=g=\#G$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U129` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §10, source L21978 | A character $\chi$ is called irreducible if it belongs to an irreducible representation. Every central function $\varphi$ can be written uniquely as a linear combination $$ \varphi = \sum c _ {\chi} \chi , \quad c _ {\chi} \in \mathbb {C}, $$ of irreducible characters. $\varphi$ is a character of a representation of G if and only if the $c_{\chi}$ are rational integers $\geq 0$ . For instance, for the character $r_{G}$ of the regular representation we find $$ r _ {G} = \sum \chi (1) \chi , $$ where $\chi$ varies over all irreducible characters of G. Given any two central functions $\varphi$ and $\psi$ of G, we put $$ (\varphi , \psi) = \frac {1}{g} \sum_ {\sigma \in G} \varphi (\sigma) \overline {{\psi}} (\sigma), \quad g = \# G, $$ where $\overline{\psi}$ is the function which is the complex conjugate of $\psi$ . For two irreducible characters $\chi$ and $\chi'$ , this gives | — |
| `FC09-C07-U130` | Frobenius Reciprocity (10.2) | Chapter VII, §10, source L22024 | (10.2) Frobenius Reciprocity. For every central function $\psi$ on H there is one and only one central function $h_{*}(\psi)$ on G such that one has $$ \left(\varphi , h _ {*} (\psi)\right) = \left(h ^ {*} (\varphi), \psi\right) $$ for all central functions $\varphi$ on $G$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U131` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §10, source L22034 | a) $H$ is a subgroup of $G$ and $h$ is inclusion. In this case we write $\varphi\|H$ or simply $\varphi$ instead of $h^{*}(\varphi)$ , and $\psi_{*}$ instead of $h_{*}(\psi)$ (the induced function). If $\varphi$ is the character of a representation $(\rho,V)$ of G, then $\varphi\|H$ is the character of the representation $(\rho\|H,V)$ . If $\psi$ is the character of a representation $(\rho,V)$ of H, then $\psi_{*}$ is the character of the representation $(\operatorname{ind}(\rho),\operatorname{Ind}_{G}^{H}(V))$ given by the induced G-module $$ \operatorname{Ind} _ {G} ^ {H} (V) = \left\{f: G \to V \mid f (\tau x) = \tau f (x) \quad \text {for all} \tau \in H \right\} $$ on which $\sigma \in G$ acts by $(\sigma f)(x) = f(x\sigma)$ (see chap. IV, §7). One has $$ \psi_ {*} (\sigma) = \sum_ {\tau} \psi (\tau \sigma \tau^ {- 1}), $$ where $\tau$ varies over a system of representatives on the right of G/H, and we put $\psi(\tau\sigma\tau^{-1})=0$ if $\tau\sigma\tau^{-1}\notin H$ . b) $G$ is a quotient group $H / N$ of $H$ and $h$ is the projection. We then write $\varphi$ instead of $h^{*}(\varphi)$ , and $\psi_{\natural}$ instead of $h_{*}(\psi)$ . One has $$ \psi_ {\natural} (\sigma) = \frac {1}{\# N} \sum_ {\tau \mapsto \sigma} \psi (\tau). $$ If $\varphi$ is the character of a representation $(\rho, V)$ of G, then $h^{*}(\varphi)$ is the character of the representation $(\rho \circ h, V)$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U132` | Brauer's Theorem (10.3) | Chapter VII, §10, source L22062 | (10.3) Brauer's Theorem. Every character $\chi$ of a finite group $G$ is a $\mathbb{Z}$ -linear combination of characters $\chi_{i*}$ induced from characters $\chi_i$ of degree 1 associated to subgroups $H_i$ of $G$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U133` | Proposition (10.4) | Chapter VII, §10, source L22074 | (10.4) Proposition. (i) For the principal character $\chi = 1$ , one has $$ \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \mathbf {1}, s) = \zeta_ {K} (s). $$ (ii) If $\chi, \chi'$ are two characters of $G(L\|K)$ , then $$ \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi + \chi^ {\prime}, s) = \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi , s) \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi^ {\prime}, s). $$ (iii) For a bigger Galois extension $L' \| K, L' \supseteq L \supseteq K$ , and a character $\chi$ of $G(L\|K)$ one has $$ \mathcal {L} (L ^ {\prime} \| K, \chi , s) = \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi , s). $$ (iv) If $M$ is an intermediate field, $L \supseteq M \supseteq K$ , and $\chi$ is a character of $G(L\|M)$ , then $$ \mathcal {L} (L \| M, \chi , s) = \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi_ {*}, s). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U134` | Corollary (10.5) | Chapter VII, §10, source L22184 | (10.5) Corollary. One has $$ \zeta_ {L} (s) = \zeta_ {K} (s) \prod_ {\chi \neq \mathbf {1}} \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi , s) ^ {\chi (1)}, $$ where $\chi$ varies over the nontrivial irreducible characters of $G(L\|K)$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U135` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §10, source L22220 | Composing with the Artin symbol $\left(\frac{L\|K}{\cdot}\right)$ , this gives a character of the ray class group $J^{\dagger}/P^{\dagger}$ , i.e., a Dirichlet character mod f. It induces a character on $J^{\dagger}$ , which we denote by $$ \widetilde {\chi}: J ^ {\mathfrak {f}} \to \mathbb {C} ^ {*}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U136` | Theorem (10.6) | Chapter VII, §10, source L22228 | (10.6) Theorem. Let $L\|K$ be an abelian extension, let $\mathfrak{f}$ be the conductor of $L\|K$ , let $\chi \neq 1$ be an irreducible character of $G(L\|K)$ , and $\widetilde{\chi}$ the associated Größencharakter mod $\mathfrak{f}$ . Then the Artin L-series for the character $\chi$ and the Hecke L-series for the Größencharakter $\widetilde{\chi}$ satisfy the identity $$ \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi , s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \in S} \frac {1}{1 - \chi (\varphi_ {\mathfrak {P}}) \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {p}) ^ {- s}} L (\widetilde {\chi}, s), $$ where $S = \{\mathfrak{p}\|\mathfrak{f}\|\chi (I_{\mathfrak{P}}) = 1\}$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U137` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §11, source L22284 | The discriminant $\partial = \partial_{L\|K}$ of a Galois extension $L\|K$ of algebraic number fields admits a fine structure based on group theory. It is expressed by a product decomposition $$ \mathfrak {d} = \prod f (\chi) ^ {\chi (1)}, $$ where $\chi$ varies over the irreducible characters of the Galois group $G = G(L\|K)$ . The ideals $\mathfrak{f}(\chi)$ are given by $$ \mathfrak {f} (\chi) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \nmid \infty} \mathfrak {p} ^ {f _ {\mathfrak {p}} (\chi)} $$ with $$ f _ {\mathfrak {p}} (\chi) = \sum_ {i \geq 0} \frac {g _ {i}}{g _ {0}} \operatorname{codim} V ^ {G _ {i}}, $$ where V is a representation with character $\chi$ , $G_{i}$ is the i-th ramification group of $L_{P}\|K_{p}$ , and $g_{i}$ denotes its order. This discovery goes back to EMIL ARTIN and HELMUT HASSE. The ideals $f(\chi)$ are called Artin conductors. They play an important rôle in the functional equation of the Artin L-series, which we are going to prove in the next section. Here we collect the properties needed for this, following essentially the treatment given by J.-P.SERRE in [122]. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U138` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §11, source L22316 | One has $i_G(\tau \sigma \tau^{-1}) = i_G(\sigma)$ , and $i_H(\sigma) = i_G(\sigma)$ for every subgroup $H \subseteq G$ . If $L\|K$ is unramified, then $i_G(\sigma) = 0$ for all $\sigma \in G$ , $\sigma \neq 1$ . We put $$ a _ {G} (\sigma) = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} - f i _ {G} (\sigma) & \mathrm{for} \sigma \neq 1, \\ f \sum_ {\tau \neq 1} i _ {G} (\tau) & \mathrm{for} \sigma = 1. \end{array} \right. $$ $a_{G}$ is a central function on $G$ , and we have $$ (a _ {G}, \mathbf {1} _ {G}) = \frac {1}{\# G} \sum_ {\sigma \in G} a _ {G} (\sigma) = 0. $$ We may therefore write $$ a _ {G} = \sum_ {\chi} f (\chi) \chi , \quad f (\chi) \in \mathbb {C}, $$ with $\chi$ varying over the irreducible characters of G. Our chief problem is to prove that the coefficients $f(\chi)$ are rational integers $\geq 0$ . Once we have shown this, we may form the ideal $f_{\mathfrak{p}}(\chi) = \mathfrak{p}^{f(\chi)}$ , which will be the p-component of the global Artin conductor that we want. First we prove that the function $a_{G}$ satisfies the following properties (we use the notation of the preceding section). | — |
| `FC09-C07-U139` | Proposition (11.1) | Chapter VII, §11, source L22336 | (11.1) Proposition. (i) If $H$ is a normal subgroup of $G$ , then $$ a _ {G / H} = (a _ {G}) _ {\natural}. $$ (ii) If $H$ is any subgroup of $G$ , and if $K'$ is the fixed field with discriminant $\mathfrak{d}_{K'\|K} = \mathfrak{p}^{\nu}$ , then $$ a _ {G} \| H = \nu r _ {H} + f _ {K ^ {\prime} \| K} a _ {H}. $$ (iii) Let $G_{i}$ be the $i$ -th ramification group of $G$ , $u_{i}$ the augmentation character of $G_{i}$ , and $(u_{i})_{*}$ the character of $G$ induced from $u_{i}$ . Then one has $$ a _ {G} = \sum_ {i = 0} ^ {\infty} \frac {1}{\left(G _ {0} : G _ {i}\right)} \left(u _ {i}\right) _ {*}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U140` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §11, source L22412 | $g = \# G$ . For any central function $\varphi$ of $G$ , we put $$ f (\varphi) = (\varphi , a _ {G}) $$ and $$ \varphi (G _ {i}) = \frac {1}{g _ {i}} \sum_ {\sigma \in G _ {i}} \varphi (\sigma), \quad g _ {i} = \# G _ {i}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U141` | Proposition (11.2) | Chapter VII, §11, source L22424 | (11.2) Proposition. (i) If $\varphi$ is a central function on the quotient group $G / H$ , and $\varphi'$ is the corresponding central function on $G$ , then $$ f (\varphi) = f \left(\varphi^ {\prime}\right). $$ (ii) If $\varphi$ is a central function on a subgroup $H$ of $G$ , and $\varphi_{*}$ is the central function induced by $\varphi$ on $G$ , then $$ f (\varphi_ {*}) = v _ {K} \left(\mathfrak {d} _ {K ^ {\prime} \| K}\right) \varphi (1) + f _ {K ^ {\prime} \| K} f (\varphi). $$ (iii) For a central function $\varphi$ on $G$ , one has $$ f (\varphi) = \sum_ {i \geq 0} \frac {g _ {i}}{g _ {0}} (\varphi (1) - \varphi (G _ {i})). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U142` | Proposition (11.3) | Chapter VII, §11, source L22467 | (11.3) Proposition. Let $\chi$ be a character of $G$ of degree 1. Let $j$ be the biggest integer such that $\chi\|_{G_j} \neq \mathbf{1}_{G_j}$ (when $\chi = \mathbf{1}_G$ we put $j = -1$ ). Then we have $$ f (\chi) = \eta_ {L \| K} (j) + 1, $$ and this is a rational integer $\geq 0$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U143` | Theorem (11.4) | Chapter VII, §11, source L22517 | (11.4) Theorem. If $\chi$ is a character of the Galois group $G = G(L\|K)$ , then $f(\chi)$ is a rational integer $\geq 0$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U144` | Definition (11.5) | Chapter VII, §11, source L22519 | (11.5) Definition. We define the (local) Artin conductor of the character $\chi$ of $G = G(L\|K)$ to be the ideal $$ \mathfrak {f} _ {\mathfrak {p}} (\chi) = \mathfrak {p} ^ {f (\chi)}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U145` | Proposition (11.6) | Chapter VII, §11, source L22533 | (11.6) Proposition. Let $L\|K$ be a Galois extension of local fields, and let $\chi$ be a character of $G(L\|K)$ of degree 1. Let $L_{\chi}$ be the fixed field of the kernel of $\chi$ , and $\{$ the conductor of $L_{\chi}\|K$ . Then one has $$ f = f _ {p} (\chi). $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U146` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §11, source L22547 | We now leave the local situation, and suppose that $L\|K$ is a Galois extension of global fields. Let $\mathfrak{p}$ be a prime ideal of $K$ , $\mathfrak{P}\| \mathfrak{p}$ a prime ideal of $L$ lying above $\mathfrak{p}$ . Let $L_{\mathfrak{P}}\|K_{\mathfrak{p}}$ be the completion of $L\|K$ , and $G_{\mathfrak{P}} = G(L_{\mathfrak{P}}\|K_{\mathfrak{p}})$ the decomposition group of $\mathfrak{P}$ over $K$ . We denote the function $a_{G_{\mathfrak{P}}}$ on $G_{\mathfrak{P}}$ by $a_{\mathfrak{P}}$ , and extend it to $G = G(L\|K)$ by zero. The central function $$ a _ {\mathfrak {p}} = \sum_ {\mathfrak {P} \| \mathfrak {p}} a _ {\mathfrak {P}} $$ immediately turns out to be the function $(a_{\mathfrak{P}})_*$ induced by $a_{\mathfrak{P}}\|G_{\mathfrak{P}}$ . It is therefore the character of a representation of $G$ . If now $\chi$ is a character of $G$ , then we put $$ f (\chi , \mathfrak {p}) = (\chi , a _ {\mathfrak {p}}) = f (\chi \| G _ {\mathfrak {P}}). $$ Then $\mathfrak{f}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\chi) = \mathfrak{p}^{f(\chi, \mathfrak{p})}$ is the Artin conductor of the restriction of $\chi$ to $G_{\mathfrak{P}} = G(L_{\mathfrak{P}} \| K_{\mathfrak{p}})$ . In particular, we have $\mathfrak{f}_{\mathfrak{p}}(\chi) = 1$ if p is unramified. We define the (global) Artin conductor of $\chi$ to be the product $$ f (\chi) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \nmid \infty} f _ {\mathfrak {p}} (\chi). $$ Whenever precision is called for, we write $f(L\|K,\chi)$ instead of $f(\chi)$ . The properties (11.2) of the numbers $f(\chi,\mathfrak{p})$ transfer immediately to the Artin conductor $f(\chi)$ , and we obtain the | `FC09-C07-U141` |

| `FC09-C07-U155` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §12, source L22683 | The first task is to complete the Artin $L$ -series $$ \mathcal {L} (L \| K, \chi , s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p} \nmid \infty} \frac {1}{\det \left(1 - \varphi_ {\mathfrak {P}} \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {p}) ^ {- s} ; V ^ {I _ {\mathfrak {P}}}\right)}, $$ for the character $\chi$ of $G = G(L\|K)$ , by the appropriate gamma factors. (i) $\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}(L\|K,\chi +\chi ',s) = \mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}(L\|K,\chi ,s)\mathcal{L}_{\mathfrak{p}}(L\|K,\chi ',s).$ (ii) If $L' \| K$ is a Galois subextension of $L \| K$ and $\chi$ a character of $G(L' \| K)$ , then $$ \mathcal {L} _ {\mathfrak {p}} (L \| K, \chi , s) = \mathcal {L} _ {\mathfrak {p}} (L ^ {\prime} \| K, \chi , s). $$ (iii) If $K'$ is an intermediate field of $L\|K$ and $\chi$ a character of $G(L\|K')$ , then $$ \mathcal {L} _ {\mathfrak {p}} (L \| K, \chi_ {*}, s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {q} \| \mathfrak {p}} \mathcal {L} _ {\mathfrak {q}} (L \| K ^ {\prime}, \chi , s), $$ where q varies over the places of $K'$ lying above p. | — |

| `FC09-C07-U165` | Theorem (13.2) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23073 | (13.2) Theorem. Let $\mathfrak{m}$ be a module of $K$ and $H^{\mathfrak{m}}$ an ideal group such that $J^{\mathfrak{m}} \supseteq H^{\mathfrak{m}} \supseteq P^{\mathfrak{m}}$ with index $h_{\mathfrak{m}} = (J^{\mathfrak{m}}: H^{\mathfrak{m}})$ . For every class $\mathfrak{K} \in J^{\mathfrak{m}} / H^{\mathfrak{m}}$ , the set $P(\mathfrak{K})$ of prime ideals in $\mathfrak{K}$ has density $$ d (P (\mathfrak {K})) = \frac {1}{h _ {\mathfrak {m}}} \cdot $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U166` | Lemma (13.3) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23083 | (13.3) Lemma. Let $\chi$ be a nontrivial (irreducible) character of $J^{\mathfrak{m}} / P^{\mathfrak{m}}$ (i.e., a character of degree 1). Then the Hecke $L$ -series $$ L (\chi , s) = \prod_ {\mathfrak {p}} \frac {1}{1 - \chi (\mathfrak {p}) \mathfrak {N} (\mathfrak {p}) ^ {- s}} $$ $(\chi (\mathfrak{p}) = 0$ for $\mathfrak{p}\|\mathfrak{m})$ satisfies $$ L (\chi , 1) \neq 0. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U167` | Definition / construction | Chapter VII, §13, source L23131 | Relating the prime ideals p of a class of $J^{m}/P^{m}$ , via the class field theory isomorphism $J^{m}/P^{m} \cong G(L\|K)$ , to the Frobenius automorphisms $\varphi_{\mathfrak{p}} = \left(\frac{L\|K}{\mathfrak{p}}\right)$ , gives us a Galois-theoretic interpretation of the Dirichlet density theorem. We now deduce a more general density theorem which is particularly important in that it concerns arbitrary Galois extensions (not necessarily abelian). For every $\sigma \in G(L\|K)$ , let us consider the set $$ P _ {L \| K} (\sigma) $$ of all unramified prime ideals $\mathfrak{p}$ of $K$ such that there exists a prime ideal $\mathfrak{P}\|\mathfrak{p}$ of $L$ satisfying $$ \sigma = \left(\frac {L \| K}{\mathfrak {P}}\right), $$ where $\left(\frac{L\|K}{\mathfrak{P}}\right)$ is the Frobenius automorphism $\varphi_{P}$ of P over K. It is clear that this set depends only on the conjugacy class $$ \langle \sigma \rangle = \left\{\tau \sigma \tau^ {- 1} \mid \tau \in G (L \| K) \right\} $$ of $\sigma$ and that one has $P_{L\|K}(\sigma) \cap P_{L\|K}(\tau) = \emptyset$ if $\langle \sigma \rangle \neq \langle \tau \rangle$ . What is the density of the set $P_{L\|K}(\sigma)$ ? The answer to this question is given by the Čebotarev density theorem. | — |
| `FC09-C07-U168` | Theorem (13.4) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23151 | (13.4) Theorem. Let $L\|K$ be a Galois extension with group $G$ . Then for every $\sigma \in G$ , the set $P_{L\|K}(\sigma)$ has a density, and it is given by $$ d (P _ {L \| K} (\sigma)) = \frac {\# \langle \sigma \rangle}{\# G}. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U169` | Convention / notation | Chapter VII, §13, source L23201 | Let $L\|K$ be a finite extension of algebraic number fields. We denote by $P(L\|K)$ the set of all unramified prime ideals $\mathfrak{p}$ of K which admit in L a prime divisor $\mathfrak{P}$ of degree 1 over K. So, if $L\|K$ is Galois, then $P(L\|K)$ is just the set of all prime ideals of K which split completely in L. | — |

| `FC09-C07-U171` | Corollary (13.6) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23211 | (13.6) Corollary. If $L\|K$ is an extension of degree $n$ , then the set $P(L\|K)$ has density $d(P(L\|K)) \geq \frac{1}{n}$ . | `FC09-C07-U172` | Corollary (13.7) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23237 | (13.7) Corollary. If almost all prime ideals split completely in the finite extension $L\|K$ , then $L = K$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U173` | Corollary (13.8) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23247 | (13.8) Corollary. An extension $L\|K$ is Galois if and only if every prime ideal in $P(L\|K)$ splits completely in $L$ . | — |
| `FC09-C07-U174` | Proposition (13.9) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23257 | (13.9) Proposition (M. BAUER). If $L\|K$ is Galois and $M\|K$ is an arbitrary finite extension, then $$ P (L \| K) \dot {\supseteq} P (M \| K) \iff L \subseteq M. $$ | — |
| `FC09-C07-U175` | Corollary (13.10) | Chapter VII, §13, source L23271 | (13.10) Corollary. A Galois extension $L\|K$ is uniquely determined by the set $P(L\|K)$ of prime ideals which split completely in it. | — |
